The orchestrate skill's wall-clock rule: each busy peer's next dispatch is written before its turn ends. On the idle event, read the peer's REPORT, adjust only what the report changes, send the prompt, and delete its section here.

Every prompt ends with the standing lines: work only in your cwd on main in the shared checkout; commit as lanmower only, no trailer or attribution line; Phase A (code, docs, tests, listed in docs/PENDING_VALIDATION.md, committed locally) then Phase B (build once, witness, batch fixes, rerun only what failed, push once, one status check at most); treat any issue as a defect; stop processes by their own PID only; wait on a job once with a timeout sized from its recorded wall time; read git state once per phase; record witness wall times; `REPORT: finished|open|blocked — <summary> — witness: <output evidence>`.

## Quiet window: restart the airc server on the rebuilt ccbridge

Peers whose turn ran longer than thirty minutes were retired within a minute or two of the turn ending (gm, spoint, jayzee and fx on 2026-11-10), and any job they had left running died with them. Cause: the hub's idle clock was last set when the prompt was submitted, so a long turn was already past the 1800 s reap limit when the dispatcher's client detached. Fixed in airc/src/server/server.cpp (detach now resets the clock) and built into build/ccbridge. The running servers (pids 884614 and 931565, the older .orch/ccbridge/v0.1.0.4 binary) still have the defect. When no peer is busy, restart the server on build/ccbridge and resume each peer's newest session. Until then: answer a finished turn within a minute, resume with `resume: true` when a peer is gone, and ask peers to keep the turn open while their own job runs.

## gm: skill change for repeated work, turns and tokens (send when no peer is busy, session 57a3f21e)

We want gm to change its own skill for five waste patterns seen in this session, in one Phase A pass, then one Phase B, and push once.

1. A reachable ranked item is built in the same pass that ranks it. Do not defer it to a later turn. (Turn 4e0454188aa2 ranked run-code-background-visibility as reachable, then did not build it, and it took a second turn.)
2. A held item is reported once, with its cause. Later reports name it in one line, not in full.
3. A failing check is rerun alone. The full set runs once at the end of the pass, not after each fix.
4. A job the agent starts is waited on by its PID with a bounded timeout, in the same turn. No Monitor is armed for its own job, and a turn does not end while the job runs.
5. Status reads are bounded: the summary and the failing rows, not whole logs or pane dumps.

## fx: native orchestration, the whole of gm and the whole of hyperagent (owner direction 2026-11-11, send on fx's idle event, session 255b1fc0-52f2-4fa7-b86b-55fae17602e6)

"we want fx to implement the entire gm functionality and the entire hyperagent orchestration natively, so fx runs its full orchestration with no run-time use of gm or hyperagent. Route: separate gm's tooling minimally rather than replacing how fx works, and add no new tool surface. Find the gm-specific paths in fx, move them behind one seam with the same behaviour, and implement each gm behaviour inside that seam natively, so fx needs no gm binary, path or call at run time. Phase A for the stretch, one Phase B and one push. The native orchestration covers: a dispatch ledger held as structured state and written at each send and each turn end; the REPORT line parsed at turn end into finished, open or blocked, with the witness line kept; queued prompts for a busy root sent on its idle event; a check after each send that the instruct record reached the root; a job wait by PID with a bound, with the job's result in the continuation; resume by full session id, with a short id resolved to the full id before the send; a measurement report of busy share, early turn ends, gaps between a turn's end and its next prompt, and repeated commands with their wall time; one status call that gives repo sync, unpushed counts and host load; a stale-entry check on the ledger; and the gm skill's iteration habits built in. Use gm and hyperagent only as references for the parity rows. Verify with a witness that the run makes no call to gm or hyperagent. REPORT names the longest step and its wall time."

- 2026-11-11 16:37 UTC: owner: "decide and instruct the best solution" for a next_context wait that a client cancel cannot stop. Decision: a client cancel ends the wait. Gm is busy (task bpoqbgoxp, sent 16:36), so this is queued. Ready prompt for gm's next idle event, sent with `orch prompt gm --timeout 14400` in the background:
  "we want a client cancel to end a `next_context` wait; the owner asked the dispatcher to decide this, and this is the decision. When the host sends `notifications/cancelled` for a `next_context` call that is waiting, gm stops the wait at once, sends no reply for that request (as the protocol says), and releases the turn, so the session's next call is answered without waiting behind it. Nothing is lost: an event that was pending or arrives later stays queued and is delivered by the next `next_context`, exactly once; the run's step, open rows and jobs are unchanged; no event is consumed by a cancelled wait. The cancel is written to the run log with the request id, who sent it and the reason given, and the next `next_context` reply says in one field that the previous wait was cancelled. The same holds across the relay: a cancelled wait is dropped from the resend list and recorded, never resent after a hand-over. A cancel for a call that already answered, or for an unknown id, changes nothing and is recorded. Apply the same rule to the other waits a session can sit in (`job_status` with a wait, a `run_code` body waiting on a job): a cancel ends the wait, the job itself keeps running and stays reachable by its id. Do it in native and in the Node reference, add it to the parity contract, and witness it on your own session as well as in the sandbox: a wait cancelled mid-way, the next call answered at once, an event raised during the cancelled wait delivered once by the following `next_context`. One Phase A, one Phase B, one push through `ship.sh --push`, install last. End with REPORT: finished|open|blocked — rationale — witness: output evidence."

- Finding, pw's lead confirmed against gm's code: the pid-ledger reaper kills PIDs it cannot verify. native/components/proc/proc.cpp reap_ledger (lines 235-249) skips a line only when the recorded start time is set, and both recorded and current start times are readable, and they differ. A line with no start time, or a process whose start time reads -1, falls through to kill_tree(pid). A reused PID number is then killed. Pw's jobs run through gm's run_code, so its gate and browser PIDs go into gm's jobs/*.pids ledgers. That fits the lifecycle failure: the browser and gate vanished with no stop logged.
- Decision: the defect is gm's and gm owns the fix. Ready prompt for gm, sent at its next idle: kill only when the recorded start time is valid and equals the current one; never kill on unknown identity; forget a ledger line when its process exits. Witnesses: a process another run started survives another run's reap, and a line whose PID was reused survives. Fold it into gm's run_code reaping item in its next Phase A. Gm's current Phase B is suspect until this is in, and its "server exited" failure may be the same reaper.
- Pw does not edit gm's repo. Its evidence stays in its journal; the dispatcher relays the finding to gm at pw's turn end.
